.\v033_codex_diagnosis.txt:15609:`clear(fc1_local)` -> 遍历所有 `hidden_tile` 加载 input/gate weight -> gate GEMM 累加 -> SiLU(gate) 写 `gate_activated_shared(fp16)` -> `clear(fc1_local)` -> 遍历所有 `hidden_tile` 重新加载 input/up weight -> up GEMM 累加 -> `up * gate_activated_shared` 写 `up_activated_shared(fp16)` -> copy 到 `up_activated_local` -> load `down_shared` for `(by, expert_tile)` -> FC2 GEMM 累加进 `out_local`。  
.\v033_codex_diagnosis.txt:15613:`out_local`：仍在 expert loop 外 clear，正确，因为 FC2 需要跨所有 expert tiles 累加。

.\v039_codex_diagnosis.txt:17180:源码结构见 [v038_fp32_workspace_exp2.py](D:/C_data/EurakaOpts/contest_fused_moe_gemm/v038_fp32_workspace_exp2.py:20)：`block_dexpert=128`、`threads=256`、统一 `num_stages=1`；FC1 loop 在约 78 行，FC2 loop 在约 170 行；workspace 是外部 cached `torch.empty(..., dtype=torch.float32)`。
.\v039_codex_diagnosis.txt:17205:中低。FC2 显式 shared 只有 `down_shared = (128,128) fp16 = 32 KiB`；双 stage 估算 down shared 约 `64 KiB`，比 FC1 的 gate/up 两套 shared 更可控。FC1 若 stage=2，gate+up 两套权重本身已约 `64 KiB`，pipeline 双缓冲可能到 `128 KiB`，风险明显更高。
.\v039_codex_diagnosis.txt:17211:case1：可能小幅改善或持平；`intermediate=8192`，FC2 K loop 很长，隐藏 workspace/down copy 延迟有空间。

(1.0 + T.exp2(-gate_value * scale)))\n                    up_activated_shared[i, j] = up_local[i, j] * gate_local[i, j]\n\n                for i, j in T.Parallel(block_token, block_expert):\n num_blocks_m=num_blocks_m,\n        )\n        _KERNEL_CACHE[key] = kernel\n    kernel(\n        stacked_expert_tokens,\n        gate_w,\n        up_w,\n        down_w,\n        routed_expert_weights,\n        group_sizes,\n        group_offsets,\n        group_padded_offsets,\n        group_idx_for_bx,\n        out,\n    )\n",

.\v042_codex_diagnosis.txt:2509:.\contest_fused_moe_gemm\v031_codex_verify.txt:18:验证：正式d_expert为8192或2048，均可被32整除；expert_tile循环次数翻倍但覆盖集合不变；FP32 FC1和FC2按32宽分块，FC2仍累加全部expert维；shared从64KiB降为32KiB（四个与block_expert相关的fp16 shared各减半）；与block_expert相关fragment减半；out_local/input fragment不变。
.\v042_codex_diagnosis.txt:2555:.\contest_fused_moe_gemm\v030_codex_diagnosis.txt:25:共同结构仍是：一个T.Kernel中按expert_tile循环，先两次FC1 GEMM累加gate/up，elementwise写up_activated_shared，再复制到up_activated_local，随后FC2 GEMM累加out_local；一个program block同时持有gate_local、up_local、up_activated_local、out_local和64KiB shared。
.\v042_codex_diagnosis.txt:2556:.\contest_fused_moe_gemm\v030_codex_diagnosis.txt:26:官方参考使用同一prim_func里的两个顺序T.Kernel launch，并通过外部up_logits workspace连接；它不会把FC1/FC2 fragments同时置于同一program block生命周期。但十参数接口没有workspace参数，不允许torch分配workspace，也不应假设T.alloc_global可用。
.\v042_codex_diagnosis.txt:2557:.\contest_fused_moe_gemm\v030_codex_diagnosis.txt:34:B 删除`up_activated_local`，尝试FC2直接以`up_activated_shared`作为A operand（如果TileLang GEMM layout合法），隔离fragment复制并减fragment资源。

The optimization log already has a closely related custom grouped FC2 failure at v153 and the v160 baseline. I’m checking the latest log tail and source names to see whether v163 has an explicit local entry or only the OJ result you provided.

### v146_e16_up_slab_stream_fc1.py

- Parent: v141.
- E16-only architecture: retain full gate BMM but split up BMM into two N4096 slabs, applying guarded SwiGLU after each slab to halve peak `up_output` memory.
- Codex implementation and C500 correctness gates passed all official shapes.
- XPUOJ submission 105913: Accepted, score 83.67, at 2.121 / 3.668 / 6.572 ms (sample case1 2.123).
- Changed E16 regressed 0.097 ms (+4.79%); E32/E64 movement is noise. Aggregate time regressed 0.64%.
- Conclusion: rollback and block FC1 output-slab streaming. Lower peak memory does not offset extra BMM/SwiGLU launches and worse slab GEMM efficiency.

### v147_e32_static_m176.py

- Parent: v141.
- Evidence correction and experiment: v114 dynamic M176 had been blocked by forbidden `.item()` rather than a numerical failure, so v147 tried a host-sync-free static E32 `expert_block_m=176` based on the C500 harness observing max group size170.
- Codex implementation and C500 all-shape numerical gates passed.
- XPUOJ submission 106723 failed on unchanged E16, so Codex allowed one unchanged retry.
- Retry submission 106729: E16/E64 Accepted at 2.043/6.634 ms, but changed E32 WrongAnswer with a missing valid output (`target=0`, `ref=3.408203125`).
- Conclusion: the OJ E32 routing distribution is not bounded by the local harness's observed max170; static M176 truncates valid rows. Roll back and block all static M<192 specialization unless the official contract provides a hard bound.

### v147_e32_static_m176.py

- Parent: v141.
- Evidence correction and experiment: v114 dynamic M176 had been blocked by forbidden `.item()` rather than a numerical failure, so v147 tried a host-sync-free static E32 `expert_block_m=176` based on the C500 harness observing max group size170.
- Codex implementation and C500 all-shape numerical gates passed.
- XPUOJ submission 106723 failed on unchanged E16, so Codex allowed one unchanged retry.
- Retry submission 106729: E16/E64 Accepted at 2.043/6.634 ms, but changed E32 WrongAnswer with a missing valid output (`target=0`, `ref=3.408203125`).
- Conclusion: the OJ E32 routing distribution is not bounded by the local harness's observed max170; static M176 truncates valid rows. Roll back and block all static M<192 specialization unless the official contract provides a hard bound.

### v148_single_fullbatch_fc2.py (blocked before implementation)

- Parent would have been v141.
- The proposed all-expert full-batch FC2 was withdrawn before implementation after duplicate/contamination audit. Its E64 path is explicitly excluded because full-batch FC2 is a high-memory direction and prior snapshots contain related unsafe cached-output variants.
- Codex found no fresh candidate satisfying the current direction-switch constraints without either repeating v141-equivalent work or taking a high-memory/known-WrongAnswer path.
- No v148 source, C500 run, or XPUOJ submission was made.

### v148_single_fullbatch_fc2.py (blocked before implementation)

- Parent would have been v141.
- The proposed all-expert full-batch FC2 was withdrawn before implementation after duplicate/contamination audit. Its E64 path is explicitly excluded because full-batch FC2 is a high-memory direction and prior snapshots contain related unsafe cached-output variants.

- No v148 source, C500 run, or XPUOJ submission was made.

### v149_swiglu_group_sizes_metadata.py

- Parent: v141.
- Change: use the existing `group_sizes[e]` directly in guarded SwiGLU instead of loading `group_offsets[e+1] - group_offsets[e]`; all BMM, pack, unpack, math, cache, and workspace paths remain unchanged.
- Codex implementation and C500 gates passed all shapes.
- XPUOJ submission 106766: Accepted, score 83.67, at 2.027 / 3.670 / 6.567 ms.
- Versus v141: +0.003 / -0.025 / +0.004 ms; aggregate 12.282 -> 12.264 ms (-0.15%), low-confidence and mostly judge noise.
- Conclusion: promote as operational parent only; metadata substitution family is exhausted. Do not continue equivalent metadata swaps.

### v150_e64_four_unpack_launch.py (stopped before C500)

- Parent: v149.
- Proposed E64 launch optimization: retain four FC2 chunk outputs and replace four unpack launches with one four-input unpack kernel.
- Stopped before C500 under the direction-switch rule. Codex identified four simultaneous packed outputs, roughly 42 MiB of extra peak live memory, in exchange for only three small launch eliminations; this cannot plausibly yield the required 3-5% gain and increases OOM risk.
- No C500 or XPUOJ submission. Unpack-launch consolidation without reducing FC2 output materialization is marked low-value.

### v150_e64_four_unpack_launch.py (stopped before C500)

- Parent: v149.
- Proposed E64 launch optimization: retain four FC2 chunk outputs and replace four unpack launches with one four-input unpack kernel.
- Stopped before C500 under the direction-switch rule. Codex identified four simultaneous packed outputs, roughly 42 MiB of extra peak live memory, in exchange for only three small launch eliminations; this cannot plausibly yield the required 3-5% gain and increases OOM risk.
- No C500 or XPUOJ submission. Unpack-launch consolidation without reducing FC2 output materialization is marked low-value.

### v151_e64_grouped_fc2_pointer.py

- Parent: v149.
- Architectural pivot based on official MACA grouped/fused-MoE examples: replace only E64 chunked high-level FC2 BMM + unpack with a direct grouped TileLang FC2 kernel using 64x128x64 tiles and routed-weight output.
- Codex implementation and C500 numerical gates passed all official shapes; changed E64 max error was 0.00390625.
- XPUOJ submission 106892: Accepted, score 79.33, at 2.025 / 3.688 / 14.435 ms.
- Changed E64 regressed +7.868 ms (+119.8%, 2.20x); score fell 4.34. The custom grouped kernel cannot compete with the backend high-level BMM on this dense E64 FC2 shape.
- Conclusion: rollback and retire custom grouped FC2 entirely. Do not tune its tiles/stages/threads. Preserve backend BMM and switch to a different operator/dataflow axis.

### v152_e32_result_bmm.py

- Parent: v149.
- Operator-dispatch probe: E32-only allocation-returning `torch.bmm` for gate FC1, up FC1, and FC2; no `out=`, no custom kernels, and no memory-cache changes. This was intentionally distinct from v130's preallocated `out=` BMM.
- Codex and C500 gates passed all three shapes.
- XPUOJ submission 106915: WrongAnswer/score 56 because testcase 2 warmup failed before user code due the sandbox restriction `TorchProxyError: Access to torch.bmm is not allowed.` Cases 1 and 3 passed at 2.052 / 6.626 ms.
- Conclusion: no timing conclusion and no retry; explicit `torch.bmm` is unavailable in the OJ sandbox. Mark this dispatch API blocked and retain v149.

### v153-v156 architecture research (blocked before implementation)

- v153 investigated flattened/block-diagonal standard matmul and combined-weight FC1 revival; memory proofs showed huge block-diagonal materialization or known combined-weight OOM risk, so no implementation passed the gate.
- v154-v156 searched remote MACA/TileLang fused-MoE, grouped-GEMM, operator APIs, and web references. The available official primitives either require unavailable metadata preparation, reproduce retired custom GEMM families, or use APIs not allowed by the OJ sandbox. No source was submitted.

### v157_concurrent_fc1_streams.py

- Parent: v149.
- Architecture: launch unchanged high-level gate/up FC1 `@` matmuls concurrently on two cached CUDA streams, then join before SwiGLU.
- Codex stream-order/allocator review and C500 all-shape numerical gates passed.
- XPUOJ submission 107046 initially remained Pending with no progress for more than 20 minutes, then finished WrongAnswer during warmup: `TorchProxyError: Access to torch.cuda is not allowed.` No user kernel timing was produced.
- Conclusion: no performance claim and no retry. The OJ sandbox blocks `torch.cuda`, so concurrent-stream and CUDA-graph APIs are unavailable regardless of local C500 correctness.

### v158 controlled baseline remeasurement

- Pareto audit found no uncombined Accepted gains after v149. Two unchanged v149 control submissions established the current judge-noise envelope.
- Submission 107096: Accepted83.67 at 2.038 / 3.686 / 6.551 ms.
- Submission 107103: Accepted83.67 at 2.027 / 3.684 / 6.578 ms.
- Conclusion: v149's original 2.027 / 3.670 / 6.567 result is within normal noise; no v158 code version was created.

### v159_e64_local_combined_fc1.py

- Parent: v149.
- E64-only revival of combined FC1 with local transient combined weight, eliminating v132's persistent cross-testcase cache and deleting the 3.50 GiB combined weight before FC2.
- Codex implementation and C500 numerical gates passed. The prior generator-OOM mechanism was successfully removed.
- XPUOJ submission 107132: Accepted, score 80.67, at 2.032 / 3.696 / 11.794 ms.
- Changed E64 regressed +5.227 ms (+79.6%); the per-call 3.50 GiB weight allocation/copy dominates.
- Conclusion: rollback and permanently retire combined-weight FC1 in all persistent/transient/preallocated/block-diagonal variants.

### v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py

- Parent: v149.
- E16-only lifetime/order experiment: compute up FC1 before gate FC1, then write SwiGLU activation in-place to `up_output` and release `gate_output`; E32/E64 retain v149.
- Codex implementation and C500 numerical gates passed all official shapes.
- XPUOJ submission 107154: Accepted, score 83.67, at 2.018 / 3.673 / 6.557 ms (sample case1 2.024).
- Versus v149: -0.009 / +0.003 / -0.010 ms; aggregate -0.13%. The E16 signal is small and the unchanged paths are noise.
- Conclusion: promote as a low-confidence operational baseline, but close the FC1 order/lifetime family because it produced no score gain and only marginal timing movement.

### v161 E16 layout investigation (blocked before implementation)

- Parent would have been v160.
- Investigated zero-copy E16 input views, 128-core/64-overflow splitting, and E16 routed-weight prescaling.
- Layout proof blocked zero-copy: physical input uses irregular per-expert 128-row padded segments (`3840` physical rows versus `16*192=3072` dense rows), so no regular `[16,192,H]` view exists.
- Static two-source splitting saves no GEMM rows and adds launches; dynamic overflow compaction has no OJ-safe construction path with currently allowed APIs. E16 prescaling has an unfavorable intermediate/hidden ratio.
- No v161 source, C500 run, or XPUOJ submission.

## Current Baseline

- Operational parent: `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`, XPUOJ submission 107154, Accepted score 83.67, timings 2.018 / 3.673 / 6.557 ms.
- Historical performance references: v149 submission 106766 at 2.027 / 3.670 / 6.567 and v141 submission 105768 at 2.024 / 3.695 / 6.563.
- Measured performance envelope from v136/v141: approximately 2.02-2.03 / 3.68-3.70 / 6.56-6.57 ms.
- Historical best timing reference: `v112_e32_chunk32_fused_e64_chunk16.py`, XPUOJ submission 104790, score 83.67, timings 2.038 / 3.701 / 6.582 ms.
- Fresh v112 control submission 105048: Accepted score 83.67 at 2.060 / 3.726 / 6.626 ms; use this as a same-session noise reference.
- Timings: 2.038 / 3.701 / 6.582 ms for cases 1/2/3.
- Architecture: packed per-expert dense input with M=192; ordinary two-BMM FC1 for all cases; FC2 chunks 16/32/16 for E=16/E=32/E=64; separate SwiGLU; weighted unpack; Python kernel/workspace caching.
- `v100_packed_dense_81_baseline.py` remains the original score-81 reference, not the active baseline.
- Do not inspect or use repository `xpuoj/` or old `fused_moe_gemm/` experiments. A version counts only after a fresh current-XPUOJ Accepted result.
- C500 remains compile/numerical validation only; performance decisions come from current XPUOJ.

The source confirms the changed E64 path computes rows `:128` with standard chunked backend BMM, then sends the remaining tail rows through a custom TileLang FC2 kernel. That makes the regression attributable to the hybrid custom tail rather than broad E16/E32 or tile-size-only changes.

 144: def _e64_fc2_tail_kernel(hidden, total_padded_tokens, total_valid_tokens):

 146:     block_n = 128
 147:     block_k = 64

 150:     @T.prim_func
 151:     def kernel(
 152:         activation: T.Tensor((64, 192, 2048), T.float16),
 153:         down_w: T.Tensor((64, hidden, 2048), T.float16),
 154:         routed_expert_weights: T.Tensor((total_valid_tokens,), T.float32),
 155:         group_sizes: T.Tensor((64,), T.int32),
 156:         group_offsets: T.Tensor((65,), T.int32),
 157:         group_padded_offsets: T.Tensor((65,), T.int32),
 158:         out: T.Tensor((total_padded_tokens, hidden), T.float16),
 159:     ):
 160:         with T.Kernel(64, 4, T.ceildiv(hidden, block_n), threads=threads) as (expert_id, bm, bn):
 161:             row_base = 128 + bm * block_m
 162:             group_size = group_sizes[expert_id]
 163:             if row_base < group_size:
 164:                 input_shared = T.alloc_shared((block_m, block_k), T.float16)
 165:                 weight_shared = T.alloc_shared((block_n, block_k), T.float16)
 166:                 output_local = T.alloc_fragment((block_m, block_n), T.float32)
 167:                 T.clear(output_local)
 168:                 for ko in T.Pipelined(T.ceildiv(2048, block_k), num_stages=2):
 169:                     T.copy(
 170:                         activation[expert_id, row_base : row_base + block_m, ko * block_k : (ko + 1) * block_k],
 171:                         input_shared,
 172:                     )
 173:                     T.copy(
 174:                         down_w[expert_id, bn * block_n : (bn + 1) * block_n, ko * block_k : (ko + 1) * block_k],
 175:                         weight_shared,
 176:                     )
 177:                     T.gemm(input_shared, weight_shared, output_local, transpose_B=True)
 178:                 raw_start = group_offsets[expert_id]
 179:                 padded_start = group_padded_offsets[expert_id]
 180:                 for i, j in T.Parallel(block_m, block_n):
 181:                     if row_base + i < group_size:
 182:                         out[padded_start + row_base + i, bn * block_n + j] = (

codex
RESULT_INTEGRITY

v163 is Accepted, so this is a performance rejection, not a correctness rejection. The changed surface is E64 FC2 only: [v163_e64_hybrid_fc2_tail.py](/D:/C_data/EurakaOpts/contest_fused_moe_gemm/v163_e64_hybrid_fc2_tail.py:512) keeps standard backend BMM for rows `:128`, then runs `_e64_fc2_tail_kernel` for rows `128:192`.

TIMING_COMPARISON

Parent v160: `83.67`, `2.018 / 3.673 / 6.557 ms`, total `12.248 ms`.

v163: `79.33`, `2.024 / 3.674 / 14.205 ms`, total `19.903 ms`.

Deltas:
- E16: `+0.006 ms`, `+0.30%`, noise.
- E32: `+0.001 ms`, `+0.03%`, noise.
- E64: `+7.648 ms`, `+116.6%`, `2.17x` slower.
- Aggregate: `+7.655 ms`, `+62.5%`.
- Score: `-4.34`, about `-5.2%`.

ROOT_CAUSE

The custom tail FC2 kernel is the regression. It computes only tail rows, but still launches over `64 experts x 4 row-blocks x ceil(hidden/128)` and performs a full 2048-K GEMM loop per live block. The backend BMM remains much stronger for this dense E64 down-projection. This matches prior v151 evidence: full custom grouped E64 FC2 also regressed to `14.435 ms`, `+119.8%`.

FAMILY_STATUS

Retire the hybrid custom-tail FC2 family entirely. This is not a tile-tuning problem. Both full-custom and tail-custom variants converge to the same failure mode: custom TileLang FC2 cannot beat the backend batched matmul on E64 FC2. Do not spend attempts on `block_m/block_n/block_k/stages/threads`, head/tail thresholds, or partial custom grouped FC2 splits.

BASELINE_DECISION

Retain v160 as operational baseline: `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`, submission `107154`, Accepted `83.67`, `2.018 / 3.673 / 6.557 ms`.

REMAINING_DIRECTIONS

1. E32/E64 padding-work reduction while preserving backend BMM  
   Best evidence: FC2 padded rows remain the long bottleneck, but backend BMM is mandatory. Feasible only if shape construction stays OJ-safe and avoids `.item()` or static underbounds.

2. E32-only exact or bucketed M layout with correctness-safe fallback  
   Evidence: v147 showed potential target area but failed because static `M=176` truncated OJ routing. Revisit only with a hard-safe `M=192 fallback` path or conservative buckets.

3. FC1/SwiGLU lifetime and aliasing variants on E32  
   Evidence: E16 sequential-free was Accepted and slightly improved. Similar ordering may reduce peak/live pressure for E32 without forbidden APIs, though expected gain is modest.

4. Route-weight movement into activation for E32 or selective cases  
   Evidence: E64 prescale was Accepted and neutral, so correctness/API risk is low. Could still help a different case if it removes weighted unpack cost without harming matmul efficiency.

5. Pack/unpack layout rewrite targeting launch count and memory traffic, not GEMM replacement  
   Evidence: elementwise block tuning is saturated, but a materially different layout that removes whole pack/unpack passes may still matter. Must retain standard `@` BMM and avoid extra live FC2 outputs.

NEXT_DIRECTION

Pick direction 1: backend-BMM-preserving padding-work reduction. It attacks the real bottleneck without repeating the failed custom FC2 family, and it stays closest to APIs already accepted by OJ.

FINAL_VERDICT

v163 is rejected. Retire hybrid custom-tail E64 FC2 and the broader custom FC2 tail/head split family. Keep v160. Continue with a materially different backend-BMM-preserving padding/dataflow family rather than pausing or tuning tiles.

RESULT_INTEGRITY

v163 is Accepted, so this is a performance rejection, not a correctness rejection. The changed surface is E64 FC2 only: [v163_e64_hybrid_fc2_tail.py](/D:/C_data/EurakaOpts/contest_fused_moe_gemm/v163_e64_hybrid_fc2_tail.py:512) keeps standard backend BMM for rows `:128`, then runs `_e64_fc2_tail_kernel` for rows `128:192`.

TIMING_COMPARISON

Parent v160: `83.67`, `2.018 / 3.673 / 6.557 ms`, total `12.248 ms`.

v163: `79.33`, `2.024 / 3.674 / 14.205 ms`, total `19.903 ms`.

Deltas:
- E16: `+0.006 ms`, `+0.30%`, noise.
- E32: `+0.001 ms`, `+0.03%`, noise.
- E64: `+7.648 ms`, `+116.6%`, `2.17x` slower.
- Aggregate: `+7.655 ms`, `+62.5%`.
- Score: `-4.34`, about `-5.2%`.

ROOT_CAUSE

The custom tail FC2 kernel is the regression. It computes only tail rows, but still launches over `64 experts x 4 row-blocks x ceil(hidden/128)` and performs a full 2048-K GEMM loop per live block. The backend BMM remains much stronger for this dense E64 down-projection. This matches prior v151 evidence: full custom grouped E64 FC2 also regressed to `14.435 ms`, `+119.8%`.

FAMILY_STATUS

Retire the hybrid custom-tail FC2 family entirely. This is not a tile-tuning problem. Both full-custom and tail-custom variants converge to the same failure mode: custom TileLang FC2 cannot beat the backend batched matmul on E64 FC2. Do not spend attempts on `block_m/block_n/block_k/stages/threads`, head/tail thresholds, or partial custom grouped FC2 splits.
